is the loan market clearing condition. It is not an independent structural equation but the requirement that entrepreneur credit demand and bank credit supply coincide; equivalently, it pins the equilibrium loan rate $R^S_t$ at which the entrepreneur participation condition~\eqref{eq:A3_indiff} holds. As with sovereign bond market clearing, it is a consequence of the agents' optimality conditions rather than an addition to them.



 
% ============================================================
%  VARIABLE AND EQUATION INVENTORY — A.1 and A.5
% ============================================================
\subsection{Non-Detrended equation system}
The equilibrium in a non-detrended path is determined by equations\eqref{eq:Cisaster_AR}, \eqref{eq:A3_LOM} and \eqref{eq:SDF}-\eqref{eq:A5_labour} corresponding to the 31 variables.
 
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\toprule

\midrule

A.1.2 & Labour-leisure FOC & $L_t$ \\
A.1.3 & Deposit Euler & (jointly with A.1.6: $R^d_t=R^f_t$) \\

A.3.2.5 & Bank net worth accumulation & $N^b_t$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{A.3.3 Disaster Transmission}} \\
Eq. \eqref{eq:Cisaster_AR} & Disaster probability process & $\theta_t$ (exogenous) \\
A.3.3.1$^{\dagger}$ & Equilibrium bond pricing condition (groundwork) & --- \\
A.3.3.2/2b/2c & Resilience; $\Lambda^M$ closed form, $\tilde\Lambda^M_t$ normalised & $H^b_t$; $\tilde\Lambda^M_t$ varies with $\theta_t$ \\
A.3.3.3 & Sovereign bond price (closed form) & $Q^b_t$ \\
A.3.3.4$^{\dagger}$ & Sovereign bond return (state-contingent) & --- \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{A.4 Public Authority}} \\
A.4.1 & Government budget constraint & $T_t$ (endogenous) \\
A.4.2$^{\dagger}$ & Fisher equation (links existing variables) & --- \\
A.4.3 & Taylor rule & $r_t$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{3}{l}{\textit{A.5 Market Clearing}} \\
A.5.1 & Goods market clearing & $C_t$ \\
A.5.2 , A.5.3 & Labour market and capital services clearing & $L_t, K_t$ (consistency check) \\
% A.5.3$^{\ddagger}$ & Bond market clearing (restates A.3.2.4) & --- \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\footnote{$^{\dagger}$Theoretical groundwork; the closed-form result it justifies is what enters the implemented system. $^{\ddagger}$Restates an equation already counted elsewhere; included for narrative completeness, not as an independent equation.}
 
% No new endogenous variables are introduced in A.4--A.5 beyond those already listed: $T_t$ is residually determined by equation~\eqref{eq:A4_govBC} and does not enter any other equilibrium condition after the consolidation in A.4.1. The Fisher equation (A.4.2) connects two already-listed variables $r_t$ and $R^f_t$ rather than introducing a new one. The model is now complete in level form and ready for stationarization in Appendix~B.

% ===================================================================
% Step 2 — Stationarization (Gourio 2012 Trick)
% ===================================================================
\section{Stationarization}\label{sec:Stationarization}

This section conducts the detrending and stationarization of the models equation system. This is necessary as productivity $z_t$ grows at a rate $e^\mu$ per quarter, and therefore a generic real quantity $X_t$, will be written as $x_t \equiv X_t/z_t$ for its detrended counterpart. One exception gives the value function $V_t \equiv \tilde{V}_t^{1-\psi}$, which aggregates consumption raised to the $(1-\psi)$ power and therefore inherits $z_t^{1-\psi}$, not $z_t$. We write $v_t \equiv \tilde{V}_t/z_t$, so that $V_t = v_t^{1-\psi}z_t^{1-\psi}$. Further, \textcite{Isore2017} use the \textcite{Gourio2012} trick to eliminate the binary disaster event $x_{t+1}$ from the equilibrium system, replacing it with the smooth probability $\theta_t$. We therefore define the detrended variables 

\begin{equation*}
  c_t \equiv C_t/z_t,\quad
  k_t \equiv K_t/z_t,\quad
  i_t \equiv I_t/z_t,\quad
  y_t \equiv Y_t/z_t,\quad
  v_t \equiv \tilde V_t/z_t.
\end{equation*}
%
Rates and prices already expressed as ratios of trend quantities require no transformation, since the trend cancels automatically in a ratio of two objects growing at the same rate, thus $Q_t$, $\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$, $R^K_t, R^S_t, R^f_t, R^d_t$, $u_t$, $L_t$, $\pi_t, \pi_t^*, \Omega_t$, and $mc_t$ are stationary as written.  Real prices already deflated by $p_t$ keep their existing lowercase symbols ($w_t\equiv W_t/p_t$, $r^k_t\equiv P^k_t/p_t$); the operation in this appendix is deflation by the productivity trend $z_t$ only, and where a variable requires both the two are applied in sequence. The household and firm blocks below follow the corresponding steps in \textcite{Isore2017}; the banking block (B.15--B.28) has no template and is derived here from the level-form equations of Appendix~\ref{sec:banking_block} directly.

Three conditional moments of the disaster indicator recur throughout and are named once here.  All are instances of~\eqref{eq:gourio_star}
at different exponents $\kappa$:
%
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{D}(\theta_t)\equiv(1-\theta_t)+\theta_t(1-\Delta^k)^{1-\gamma},
  \quad
  \mathcal{E}(\theta_t)\equiv(1-\theta_t)+\theta_t(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma},
  \quad
  \mathcal{K}(\theta_t)\equiv 1-\theta_t\Delta^k .
\end{equation*}
%
where $\mathcal{D}$ generates the pseudo-discount factor $\beta(\theta_t)$, $\mathcal{E}$ is carried by the stochastic discount factor, and $\mathcal{K}$ by the physical capital stock.  Each reduces to unity at $\Delta^k=0$ or $\theta_t=0$.  They are distinct objects and are not interchangeable.
 
 % Thus, we will stationarise the (1) household's utility function, capital and capital accumulation, FOC's on consumption, labor, investment and utilization rate, (2) firms detrended  optimality conditions and production function, (3) the entrepreneurs and banks XXXXXXXXXXX as well as (4) the public authorities monetary policy rule and aggregate resource constraint.

\paragraph{\textcite{Gourio2012} Trick}
As applied in \textcite{Isore2017}, we make use of a theoretical device that makes it feasable to solve the model with perturbation methods, even if a realised diaster component is present. It eliminates the binary disaster indicator $x_{t+1}$ from every expectation in which it appears, replacing it with its conditional first moment. Since $x_{t+1} \in \{0,1\}$ is Bernoulli with $\Pr_t(x_{t+1}=1)=\theta_t$, for any constant exponent $\kappa$,
%
\begin{align*}
  \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[e^{\kappa x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}\right] 
  &= \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[(1-\Delta^k)^{\kappa x_{t+1}}\right] \\[4mm]
  &= (1-\theta_t)\cdot(1-\Delta^k)^0 + \theta_t\cdot(1-\Delta^k)^\kappa \\[4mm]
  &= (1-\theta_t)+\theta_t(1-\Delta^k)^\kappa \tag{B.$\star$}
  \label{eq:gourio_star}
\end{align*}
%
a direct consequence of $x_{t+1}$ taking only the values $0$ and $1$. Equation~\eqref{eq:gourio_star} is invoked repeatedly below with different values of $\kappa$. The pseudo-discount factor $\beta(\theta_t)$ already established in equation~\eqref{eq:betatheta} is exactly~\eqref{eq:gourio_star} evaluated at $\kappa=1-\gamma$, raised to the power $(1-\psi)/(1-\gamma)$ and scaled by $\beta_0$, which confirms that the identity reproduces an already-verified result before it is used to derive new ones.

\subsection{Households}\label{sec:stat_household}

\paragraph{Capital Accumulation.}
Starting from the level-form law of motion,
%
\begin{equation*}
  K_{t+1} = e^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}\Bigl[\left(1-\delta_0u_t^\eta\right)K_t+S\left(\frac{I_t}{K_{t}}\right)K_t\Bigr],
\end{equation*}
%
divide both sides by $z_{t+1}$. Using  \eqref{eq:prod_process}, the disaster factor on the left is 

\begin{align*}
    \frac{e^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}}{z_{t+1}} = \frac{1}{(z_te^{\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}})}
\end{align*} 
%
It cancels state by state, not merely in expectation, because capital destruction and productivity destruction share the identical realization $x_{t+1}$. This is the structural reason for assuming the same $\Delta^k$ drives both processes as it allows the indicator to vanish from a level identity without requiring an expectation at all. With  $k_t \equiv K_t/z_t$, $i_t \equiv I_t/z_t$, and noting that $I_t/K_t=i_t/k_t$ since both share the same trend,
%
\begin{equation}
  k_{t+1} = e^{-\mu-\varepsilon_{z,t+1}}\Bigl[(1-\delta_0u_t^\eta)k_t+S\left(\frac{i_t}{k_t}\right)k_t\Bigr]
  \tag{B.1}
  \label{eq:B_capital}
\end{equation}
%
The disaster indicator has left the equation entirely as no expectation operator and $\theta_t$ remain.

\paragraph{Utility function and Stochastic Discount Factor.}

Two exponent identities follow directly from $\chi\equiv1-\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\psi}$ and are used repeatedly below:
%
\begin{equation*}
  (1-\psi)(1-\chi)=1-\gamma,
  \qquad
  \chi(1-\psi)=(1-\psi)-(1-\gamma)=\gamma-\psi .
\end{equation*}
%
Detrending the level-form recursion by $z_t^{1-\psi}$ and collecting the growth
factor inside the certainty equivalent gives
%
\begin{align}
  v_t^{1-\psi} &= \bigl[c_t(1-L_t)^{\varpi}\bigr]^{1-\psi}
                  + \beta_0\,\mathcal{R}^v_t, \notag\\[2mm]
  \mathcal{R}^v_t &\equiv \Bigl(\mathbb{E}_t\Bigl[
     v_{t+1}^{(1-\psi)(1-\chi)}
     e^{(1-\psi)(1-\chi)\left[x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)+\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}\right]}
   \Bigr]\Bigr)^{\!\frac{1}{1-\chi}}
  \tag{B.2}\label{eq:B_bellman}
\end{align}
%
The first says the certainty equivalent loads the disaster factor at exponent $1-\gamma$. Applying it to $\mathcal{R}^v_t$, separating $x_{t+1}$ from the
remaining integrand, and using \eqref{eq:gourio_star} at $\kappa=1-\gamma$ together with $1/(1-\chi)=(1-\psi)/(1-\gamma)$,
%
\begin{align*}
  \mathcal{R}^v_t
  &=\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[v_{t+1}^{1-\gamma}
      e^{(1-\gamma)(\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1})}\bigr]\cdot
      \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[e^{(1-\gamma)x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}\bigr]
    \Bigr)^{\!\frac{1}{1-\chi}}\\[2mm]
  &=\Bigl(\mathcal{D}(\theta_t)\,
      \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[v_{t+1}^{1-\gamma}
      e^{(1-\gamma)(\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1})}\bigr]
    \Bigr)^{\!\frac{1-\psi}{1-\gamma}} .
\end{align*}
%
The disaster moment therefore factors out of the continuation value entirely, and the constant it leaves behind, multiplied by $\beta_0$, is
%
\begin{equation*}
  \beta_0\,\mathcal{D}(\theta_t)^{\frac{1-\psi}{1-\gamma}}
  \;=\;\beta_0\Bigl[(1-\theta_t)+\theta_t(1-\Delta^k)^{1-\gamma}\Bigr]^{\frac{1-\psi}{1-\gamma}}
  \;=\;\beta(\theta_t),
\end{equation*}
%
which is \eqref{eq:betatheta}. The pseudo-discount factor is thus not an additional assumption but the residue the disaster leaves in the Bellman equation once $x_{t+1}$ has been integrated out, and the detrended value recursion can be written
%
\begin{equation*}
  v_t^{1-\psi}=\bigl[c_t(1-L_t)^{\varpi}\bigr]^{1-\psi}
  +\beta(\theta_t)\,e^{(1-\psi)\mu}
   \Bigl(\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[v_{t+1}^{1-\gamma}\bigr]\Bigr)^{\!\frac{1-\psi}{1-\gamma}},
\end{equation*}
%
evaluated at $\varepsilon_{z,t+1}=0$ as in the baseline.
%
For the stochastic discount factor \eqref{eq:SDF}, each factor is detrended in turn. 
%
The consumption-growth term contributes $(c_{t+1}/c_t)^{-\psi}$ times a trend factor $e^{-\psi[x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)+\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}]}$. The value-function ratio, using the substitution from~\eqref{eq:B_bellman}, contributes 
%
\begin{align*}
    \frac{e^{-\chi(1-\psi)[x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)+\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}]}\cdot v_{t+1}^{-\chi(1-\psi)}}{(\mathcal{R}^v_t)^{-\chi}}
\end{align*}
%
The two trend exponents add to $\psi+\chi(1-\psi)$. Substituting $\chi \equiv 1-\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\psi}$ gives $\chi(1-\psi)=(1-\psi)-(1-\gamma)=\gamma-\psi$, so
%
\begin{equation*}
  \psi+\chi(1-\psi) = \psi+(\gamma-\psi) = \gamma.
\end{equation*}
%
Risk aversion alone survives in the cross-state scaling; the EIS parameter $\psi$ cancels here and appears only in the consumption-growth term itself.\footnote{This is the same cancellation underlying $\Lambda^M=(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}$ in equation~\eqref{eq:A3_LambdaM} - the same mechanism recurring in a structurally distinct equation.} The detrended stochastic discount factor is therefore
%
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{M}_{t,t+1} = \beta_0\left(\frac{c_{t+1}}{c_t}\right)^{\!-\psi}
  \!\!\left(\frac{1-L_{t+1}}{1-L_t}\right)^{\!\varpi(1-\psi)}
  \!e^{-\gamma[x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)+\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}]}\,
  \frac{v_{t+1}^{-\chi(1-\psi)}}{(\mathcal{R}^v_t)^{-\chi}}
  \tag{B.3}
  \label{eq:B_SDF}
\end{equation}
%
The detrended value $v_{t+1}$ is invariant to the disaster draw.  This follows from the capital law of motion~\eqref{eq:B_capital}, in which
the indicator has already cancelled state by state: the detrended state vector entering period $t+1$ does not depend on $x_{t+1}$, and neither
therefore does $v_{t+1}$.  The scaling $\tilde V_{t+1}\big|_{x_{t+1}=1}=(1-\Delta^k)\tilde V_{t+1}\big|_{x_{t+1}=0}$ used in~\eqref{eq:betatheta} applies to the level value function $\tilde V_{t+1}=v_{t+1}z_{t+1}$, where the disaster enters through $z_{t+1}$ alone.  Consequently the entire disaster loading of the stochastic discount factor in~\eqref{eq:B_SDF} is carried by the explicit trend factor $e^{-\gamma x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}$.
%
Writing $\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}$ for the no-disaster realisation ($x_{t+1}=0$),
%
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{M}^d_{t,t+1} = \mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}\,(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma x_{t+1}},
  \qquad\text{so}\qquad
  \frac{\mathcal{M}^d_{t,t+1}}{\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}}=(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}
  \tag{B.3a}
  \label{eq:B_SDF_split}
\end{equation}
%
which reproduces the disaster SDF loading $\Lambda^M$ of equation~\eqref{eq:A3_LambdaM} from the stationarised system, having derived it in Section~\ref{par:asset_specific_resilience} by a different route.  The agreement of the two derivations is the internal consistency check on this block.
 
The split in~\eqref{eq:B_SDF_split} isolates the only disaster-dependent factor in the stochastic discount factor, so any conditional moment of
$\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$ is the corresponding moment of $\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}$ multiplied by a moment of $e^{\kappa x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}$. By the conditional
independence recorded above, the two factor.

At $\kappa=-\gamma$ this gives the moment required by the deposit Euler~\eqref{eq:HHO_Deposit_Euler} and the risk-free rate~\eqref{eq:riskfree_rate},
%
\begin{equation}
  \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\right]
  = \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}\right]\mathcal{E}(\theta_t),
  \qquad
  R^f_t=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}\right]\mathcal{E}(\theta_t)}
  \tag{B.3b}
  \label{eq:B_EQ}
\end{equation}
%
Multiplying instead by the growth factor $\Gamma_{t+1}$ raises the disaster exponent by one, since $\Gamma_{t+1}$ carries $e^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}$ itself:
%
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\Gamma_{t+1}
  = \mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}\,e^{\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}}\,(1-\Delta^k)^{(1-\gamma)x_{t+1}} ,
\end{equation*}
%
so that \eqref{eq:gourio_star} applies at $\kappa=1-\gamma$ and delivers the moment required wherever a payoff that itself grows with the trend is discounted,
%
\begin{equation}
  \Theta_t\;\equiv\;\mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\Gamma_{t+1}\right]
  = \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}^{f}_{t,t+1}e^{\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}}\right]
    \mathcal{D}(\theta_t)
  \tag{B.3c}
  \label{eq:B_Theta}
\end{equation}
%
Writing $a\equiv1-\Delta^k$, the two moments satisfy $\mathcal{E}\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{D}=\theta_t(1-\theta_t)(1-a)(a^{-\gamma}-1)>0$
for every $\gamma>0$, so $\Theta_t<\mathbb{E}_t[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}]\,\mathbb{E}_t[\Gamma_{t+1}]$: the stochastic discount factor and the growth factor are negatively correlated through the shared disaster draw, and the gap widens with risk aversion. It is $\Theta_t$ that carries $\beta(\theta_t)$ in closed form: at the non-stochastic steady state $\Theta=\beta(\bar\theta)e^{(1-\psi)\mu}$, derived in Appendix~\ref{sec:D2}. Substituting $\mathcal{D}$ for $\mathcal{E}$ in~\eqref{eq:B_EQ} --- equivalently, treating the discount factor and the growth factor as independent --- would suppress roughly half of the precautionary reduction in the risk-free rate that is the defining result of the rare-disaster literature \parencite{Rietz1988,Barro2006,Gourio2012}. 

% Substituting $\mathcal{D}$ for $\mathcal{E}$ in~\eqref{eq:B_EQ} --- equivalently, treating the SDF and the growth factor as independent --- suppresses roughly half of the precautionary reduction in the risk-free rate that is the defining result of the rare-disaster literature \parencite{Rietz1988,Barro2006,Gourio2012}.

\paragraph{Labour-Leisure Condition}
Equation~\eqref{eq:HHO_C}, $\varpi C_t=w_t(1-L_t)$, is a static, within-period condition with no expectation operator and no dependence on $x_{t+1}$. Both sides carry exactly one power of $z_t$, which cancels to
%
\begin{equation}
  c_t = \frac{w_t(1-L_t)}{\varpi}
  \tag{B.4}
  \label{eq:B_HHO_C}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Capital-Goods Producer's Tobin's Q.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:CP_TobinsQ} is a static first-order condition of a producer solving a period-by-period problem: no expectation, without any $x_{t+1}$ dependence. Since $I_t/K_t=i_t/k_t$,
%
\begin{equation}
  Q_t = \frac{1}{1-\tau(i_t/k_t-\bar{i}/\bar{k})}
  \tag{B.5}
  \label{eq:B_TobinsQ}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Deposit Euler and Risk-Free Rate.}
The deposit Euler equation~\eqref{eq:HHO_Deposit_Euler} and the risk-free rate ~\eqref{eq:riskfree_rate} contains only $R_t^d$ which is by construction dimensionless and $\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$, which has been detrended in~\eqref{eq:B_SDF}, so no further detrending applies.



\subsection{Firms}\label{sec:stat_firms}
\paragraph{Real Wage and Rental Rate}
\begin{align*}
    w_tz_t &= mc_t^*(1-\alpha)z_t^{1-\alpha}\tilde{K}_t^\alpha L_t^{-\alpha} \qquad \text{with} \qquad \tilde{K}_t=\tilde{k}_tz_t \\[4mm]
    &= mc_t^*(1-\alpha)\tilde{k}_t^\alpha L_t^{-\alpha}\cdot z_t \qquad \text{resulting in }\\[4mm]
    w_t &= mc_t^*(1-\alpha)\tilde{k}_t^\alpha L_t^{-\alpha} \tag{B.6}
\end{align*}
%
The rental-rate condition follows identically. Since the exponent on $z_t$ is $(1-\alpha)+(\alpha-1)=0$, the trend cancels completely and $r^k_t$ requires no detrending:
%
\begin{equation}
  r^k_t = mc_t^*\,\alpha\,\tilde{k}_t^{\alpha-1}L_t^{1-\alpha}
  \tag{B.7}
  \label{eq:B_rental}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Effective Capital.}
Dividing the definition $\tilde{K}_t \equiv u_tK_t$ by $z_t$ yields
%
\begin{equation}
  \tilde{k}_t = u_tk_t \tag{B.8}
  \label{eq:B_Ktilde}
\end{equation}
\paragraph{Reset Inflation and Calvo Recursions}
In equation~\eqref{eq:reset_inflation}, both $\Xi_{1t}$ and $\Xi_{2t}$ trend at the same rate as $Y_t$, so their ratio is trend-free without detrending either individually first:
%
\begin{equation}
  1+\pi_t^* = \frac{\nu}{\nu-1}\frac{\xi_{1t}}{\xi_{2t}}(1+\pi_t)
  \tag{B.9}
  \label{eq:B_reset_pi}
\end{equation}
%
Writing 
%
\begin{align*}
    \Xi_{1t} =& \xi_{1t}z_t \\[4mm]
    Y_t =& y_tz_t \\[4mm]
    \Xi_{1,t+1} =& \xi_{1,t+1}z_{t+1}\\[4mm]
    =& \xi_{1,t+1}z_te^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)+\mu+\varepsilon_{z,t+1}}
\end{align*}
in equation~\eqref{eq:aux1}, every term carries exactly one factor of $z_t$, which cancels:
%
\begin{equation}
  \xi_{1t} = y_t\,mc_t^*
  +\zeta\,\mathbb{E}_t\Bigl[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\,\Gamma_{t+1}\,
    (1+\pi_{t+1})^{\nu}\,\xi_{1,t+1}\Bigr]
  \tag{B.10}
  \label{eq:B_Xi1}
\end{equation}
%
\begin{equation}
  \xi_{2t} = y_t
  +\zeta\,\mathbb{E}_t\Bigl[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\,\Gamma_{t+1}\,
    (1+\pi_{t+1})^{\nu-1}\,\xi_{2,t+1}\Bigr]
  \tag{B.11}
  \label{eq:B_Xi2}
\end{equation}
%
The growth factor appearing here is not a duplicate of anything inside
$\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$. The stochastic discount factor is a ratio of marginal
utilities and is scale-free by construction, whereas $\Xi_{1t}$ and $\Xi_{2t}$
trend with output and must be deflated; the two effects are separate and both
are present. The discount object in \eqref{eq:B_Xi1}--\eqref{eq:B_Xi2} is
therefore $\Theta_t$ of~\eqref{eq:B_Theta}, not $1/R^f_t$, and the distinction
is not cosmetic: the two differ by the moment ratio
$\mathcal{D}(\theta_t)/\mathcal{E}(\theta_t)$, which moves with $\theta_t$.
Using the capital-destruction moment $\mathcal{K}$ here instead --- the natural
guess, since $\Gamma_{t+1}$ contains one power of the disaster factor --- would
be wrong: $\mathcal{K}$ is the $\kappa=1$ moment of the disaster factor alone,
whereas the object required is the $\kappa=1-\gamma$ moment of the disaster
factor \emph{combined with} the discount factor's own $-\gamma$ loading.


Aggregate inflation and price dispersion (equations~\eqref{eq:inflation_rate} and~\eqref{eq:omega}) involve only inflation rates and a lagged dispersion measure, neither of which carries a productivity trend. They therefore enter the stationarised system unchanged:
%
\begin{equation}
  (1+\pi_t)^{1-\nu}=(1-\zeta)(1+\pi_t^*)^{1-\nu}+\zeta
  \tag{B.12}
  \label{eq:B_agg_pi}
\end{equation}
%
\begin{equation}
  \Omega_t=(1-\zeta)\left(\frac{1+\pi_t}{1+\pi^*_t}\right)^{\!\nu}+\zeta(1+\pi_t)^{\nu}\Omega_{t-1}
  \tag{B.13}
  \label{eq:B_dispersion}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Aggregate Production Function.}
Dividing equation~\eqref{eq:agg_production} by $z_t$:
%
\begin{align*}
Y_t =& \frac{\tilde{K}_t^\alpha(z_tL_t)^{1-\alpha}}{\Omega_t} \notag \\[4mm]
  y_t =&\frac{\tilde{K}_t^\alpha z_t^{-\alpha}L_t^{1-\alpha}}{\Omega_t} = \frac{(\tilde{K}_t/z_t)^\alpha L_t^{1-\alpha}}{\Omega_t} \notag \\[4mm]
  y_t =& \frac{\tilde{k}_t^\alpha L_t^{1-\alpha}}{\Omega_t} \tag{B.14}
  \label{eq:B_production}
\end{align*}

\subsection{Banking Block}
% ============================================================
% Conventions: lowercase = detrended by z_t ; Gamma_t = z_t/z_{t-1}
% P^{k,real}, Q, Q^b, u, all gross rates, H^b, Lambda^M are stationary.
% k^n_{t+1} = K_{t+1}/z_t  : capital committed at t, detrended by the
%             date-t trend (distinct from k_{t+1} = K_{t+1}/z_{t+1}).
% Rtilde^K_{t+1} : disaster-free component of the return on capital.
% ============================================================

Three groups of equations appear here. The entrepreneur block mixes trending balance-sheet quantities with stationary rates, so it requires both a $z_t$ deflation and one application of \eqref{eq:gourio_star}. The bank block is almost entirely relabeling because the leverage constraint and the value function are homogeneous of degree one in net worth. The disaster-transmission block is dimensionless throughout and passes into the stationarised system unchanged in form.

\begin{align}
% A.3.1.1  Utilisation FOC
r^k_{t+1} &= Q_{t+1}\,\delta_0\,\eta\,u_{t+1}^{\eta-1}
\tag{B.15}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.1.2  Return on capital, split into disaster factor and disaster-free part
R^K_{t+1} &= e^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}\,\tilde R^K_{t+1},
\qquad
\tilde R^K_{t+1} \equiv
  \frac{r^k_{t+1}u_{t+1}+Q_{t+1}\bigl(1-\delta_0u_{t+1}^{\eta}\bigr)}{Q_t}
\tag{B.16}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.1.6  Entrepreneur participation / external finance premium
\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[R^K_{t+1}\bigr]
  &= \bigl(1-\theta_t\Delta^k\bigr)\,\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\tilde R^K_{t+1}\bigr]
   = f\!\left(\frac{n^e_t}{Q_t\,k^n_{t+1}}\right)R^S_t
\tag{B.17}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.1.5  Entrepreneur net worth — all bracket terms dated t-1
n^e_t &= \sigma^e\,\Gamma_t^{-1}\Bigl[
   R^K_t\,Q_{t-1}k^n_t
   - R^S_{t-1}\bigl(Q_{t-1}k^n_t-n^e_{t-1}\bigr)\Bigr] + \iota^e
\tag{B.18}\label{eq:B_ne}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.1.4  Entrepreneur balance sheet
Q_t s_t &= Q_t k^n_{t+1} - n^e_t
\tag{B.19}\label{eq:B_QS}\\[4pt]
\end{align}

\noindent Four of these deserve a line. The utilisation
condition~\eqref{eq:A3_util} equates two prices and is stationary as written, so
B.15 is the level equation relabelled. B.16 splits the return on capital into
the disaster factor and a disaster-free component $\tilde R^K_{t+1}$, which is a
ratio of stationary prices; the split is what allows the indicator to be
integrated out separately in the next equation.

B.17 is that integration. Since $\tilde R^K_{t+1}$ is measurable with respect to
the disaster-free branch, \eqref{eq:gourio_star} applies at $\kappa=1$:
%
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[R^K_{t+1}\bigr]
  =\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[e^{x_{t+1}\ln(1-\Delta^k)}\bigr]\,
   \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\tilde R^K_{t+1}\bigr]
  =\bigl[(1-\theta_t)+\theta_t(1-\Delta^k)\bigr]\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\tilde R^K_{t+1}\bigr]
  =\mathcal{K}(\theta_t)\,\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\tilde R^K_{t+1}\bigr],
\end{equation*}
%
so the participation condition A.3.1.6 becomes B.17. This is the one place
$\mathcal{K}$ appears rather than $\mathcal{D}$ or $\mathcal{E}$: the payoff
being averaged is the physical capital stock itself, not a discounted claim.

B.18 requires care with the dating, because every term in the bracket is
contracted at $t-1$ while the equation is deflated by $z_t$. Writing
$k^n_t\equiv K_t/z_{t-1}$ and $n^e_{t-1}\equiv N^e_{t-1}/z_{t-1}$,
%
\begin{equation*}
  \frac{Q_{t-1}K_t}{z_t}=Q_{t-1}k^n_t\,\frac{z_{t-1}}{z_t}=Q_{t-1}k^n_t\,\Gamma_t^{-1},
  \qquad
  \frac{N^e_{t-1}}{z_t}=n^e_{t-1}\Gamma_t^{-1},
\end{equation*}
%
and since the gross rates $R^K_t$ and $R^S_{t-1}$ are already stationary, the
factor $\Gamma_t^{-1}$ is common to the whole bracket rather than attaching to
any single term. The start-up transfer scales with the trend,
$\iota^e_t=\iota^e z_t$, so it enters the detrended equation as a constant and
sits \emph{outside} the bracket. B.23 has the identical structure and inherits
the same treatment. Getting this wrong --- applying $\Gamma_t^{-1}$ to the
lagged net-worth term alone --- changes the steady-state level of net worth
without changing the Blanchard--Kahn count, so it is not caught by a
determinacy check.

B.19 follows from $Q_tK^n_{t+1}/z_t=Q_tk^n_{t+1}$ by the convention above.

% ---- A.3.2 Banks ----
\begin{align*}
% A.3.2.1  Bank balance sheet
Q_ts_t + Q^b_t b^b_t &= n^b_t + d_t
\tag{B.20}\\[4pt]
% A.3.2.3  Leverage constraint
Q_ts_t + Q^b_t b^b_t &\le \lambda_t\,n^b_t
\tag{B.21}\\[4pt]
\end{align}

\paragraph{Endogenous Leverage Multiplier.}
Equation~\eqref{eq:A323} states the leverage constraint in terms of $\lambda_t$ without specifying how the multiplier itself is determined; this is filled in here rather than in Appendix~\ref{sec:banksov}, since $\lambda_t=\nu^b_t/(\theta^b-\eta^b_t)$ requires the value-function coefficients $\nu^b_t$ and $\eta^b_t$ of equation~\eqref{eq:Banker_Value}, and both turn out to already be stationary in level form. Neither coefficient carries the productivity trend: $V^b_t(n_{j,t})=\nu_t n_{j,t}+\eta_t A_{j,t}$ expresses a value in the same units as net worth and effective assets, both of which grow at $Z_t$, so the ratios $\nu_t\equiv V^b_t/n_{j,t}$ and $\eta_t\equiv V^b_t/A_{j,t}$ are trend-free, exactly as $Q_t$, $R^S_t$ and $R^d_t$ require no detrending in equation~\eqref{eq:B_SDF} and its neighbours. The level-form recursions of equations~\eqref{eq:Banker_Value}--\eqref{eq:A323} therefore pass into the stationarized system unchanged in form, subject only to relabelling $Q_tS_t,\,Q^b_tB^b_t,\,N^b_t,\,D_t$ by their detrended counterparts $Q_ts_t,\,Q^b_tb^b_t,\,n^b_t,\,d_t$ already introduced in equation~\eqref{eq:A3_bankBS}.

A further simplification, not exploited in Appendix~\ref{sec:banksov} because it was not yet needed there, is available now that the leverage constraint fixes total effective assets: since the home-bias identity~\eqref{eq:A3_homebias} pins the portfolio split at every date, $Q_ts_{j,t}=(1-\phi)A_{j,t}$ and $Q^b_tb^b_{j,t}=\phi A_{j,t}$, the effective-asset-weighted excess return of equation~\eqref{eq:Banker_Value} collapses from a two-asset average into a single $\phi$-weighted expression depending only on aggregate rates,
\begin{equation*}
  \bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}_{t+1} \;=\; (1-\phi)\bigl(R^S_t-R^d_t\bigr)+\phi\bigl(R^b_{t+1}-R^d_t\bigr) \;=\; (1-\phi)R^S_t+\phi R^b_{t+1}-R^d_t.
\end{equation*}

Discounting proceeds at $\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$ rather than at $\Theta_t$: $\nu^b_t$ and $\eta^b_t$ price a claim to a stationary payoff --- a share of net worth or of effective assets, both already detrended --- exactly as the deposit Euler~\eqref{eq:HHO_Deposit_Euler} discounts the stationary $R^d_t$ at $\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$ rather than at $\Theta_t$, the latter being reserved for payoffs that themselves grow with the trend (equation~\eqref{eq:B_Theta}).

With these two substitutions, the banker's value-function coefficients, the survival-weighted continuation value, and the leverage multiplier they jointly determine are
%
\begin{align}
  \eta^b_t &= \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\,\Omega^b_{t+1}\,\bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}_{t+1}\right],
  \qquad
  \bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}_{t+1} \equiv (1-\phi)R^S_t+\phi R^b_{t+1}-R^d_t
  \tag{B.21a}\label{eq:B_etab}\\[6pt]
  %
  \nu^b_t &= \mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\,\Omega^b_{t+1}\,R^d_t\right]
  \tag{B.21b}\label{eq:B_nub}\\[6pt]
  %
  \Omega^b_t &= (1-\sigma^b) + \sigma^b\bigl(\nu^b_t+\lambda_t\,\eta^b_t\bigr)
  \tag{B.21c}\label{eq:B_Omegab}\\[6pt]
  %
  \lambda_t &= \frac{\nu^b_t}{\theta^b-\eta^b_t}
  \tag{B.21d}\label{eq:B_lambdat}
\end{align}
%
Equations~\eqref{eq:B_etab}--\eqref{eq:B_lambdat} form a self-contained recursive block, distinct in kind from the accumulation-type recursions elsewhere in this appendix: $\Omega^b_t$ is a static function of the \emph{current}-period $\nu^b_t,\eta^b_t,\lambda_t$, while $\eta^b_t$ and $\nu^b_t$ themselves discount the \emph{next}-period continuation value $\Omega^b_{t+1}$ --- the same object appearing one step ahead in its own defining equation. The multiplier $\lambda_t$ is consequently genuinely time-varying, matching the corrected derivation in Appendix~\ref{appsec:banksov}, rather than fixed at its steady-state value.

\begin{align}
% A.3.2.4  Home-bias identity (phi ratio unaffected by detrending)
Q^b_t b^b_t &= \frac{\phi}{1-\phi}\,Q_ts_t
\tag{B.22}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.2.5  Bank net worth — all bracket terms dated t-1; factor Gamma_t^{-1}
n^b_t
&= \sigma^b\,\Gamma_t^{-1}\Big[(R^S_{t-1}-R^d_{t-1})\,Q_{t-1}s_{t-1}
   + (R^b_t-R^d_{t-1})\,Q^b_{t-1}b^b_{t-1}
   + R^d_{t-1}\,n^b_{t-1}\Big] + \iota^b
\tag{B.23}\\[10pt]
%
% ---- A.3.3 Disaster Transmission — all dimensionless, stationary ----
% A.3.3.2 / 2b  Resilience and SDF loading
H^b_t &= 1 - \theta_t\,\Delta^b\,\tilde\Lambda^M_t,
\qquad        \tilde\Lambda^M_t = \frac{(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}}
                                {1+\theta_t[(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}-1]} \tag{B.24}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.3.3  Bond price
Q^b_t &= \frac{H^b_t}{R^f_t}
\tag{B.25}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.3.4  Realised bond return (disaster factor kept explicit)
R^b_{t+1} &= \frac{1-x_{t+1}\Delta^b}{Q^b_t}
\tag{B.26}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.3.5  Expected bond return
\mathbb{E}_t\!\big[R^b_{t+1}\big] &= \frac{1-\theta_t\Delta^b}{Q^b_t}
\tag{B.27}\\[4pt]
%
% A.3.3.6  Spread (promised-yield definition)
\mathrm{spread}_t &= \frac{1}{Q^b_t}-R^f_t
  \;\approx\; \theta_t\,\Delta^b\,\tilde\Lambda^M_t
\tag{B.28}
\end{align}
%
\noindent The bank block B.20--B.23 is relabelling: the balance sheet, the
binding leverage constraint and the home-bias identity are all linear in
quantities that share the trend $z_t$, so dividing through leaves their form
untouched, and the ratio $\phi/(1-\phi)$ is dimensionless. B.23 is the exception
only in the sense that it carries the same $\Gamma_t^{-1}$ bracket structure as
B.18, for the same reason and with the same treatment of the start-up transfer
$\iota^b_t=\iota^b z_t$.

The disaster-transmission block B.24--B.28 needs no deflation at all: resilience,
the bond price, the bond return and the spread are ratios of same-trend
quantities or pure probabilities. One step is worth making explicit. The SDF
loading of A.3.3.2 is defined as a ratio of conditional moments,
$\tilde\Lambda^M_t\equiv\mathbb{E}_t[\mathcal{M}^d_{t,t+1}]/\mathbb{E}_t[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}]$.
Using the split~\eqref{eq:B_SDF_split} for the numerator and~\eqref{eq:B_EQ} for
the denominator, the common factor $Q^{f}_{t,t+1}$ cancels and
%
\begin{equation*}
  \tilde\Lambda^M_t
  =\frac{(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}}{\mathcal{E}(\theta_t)}
  =\frac{(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}}
        {1+\theta_t\bigl[(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}-1\bigr]},
\end{equation*}
%
which is the closed form quoted in B.24. Normalising by the conditional mean
rather than by the no-disaster realisation is what makes $H^b_t$ commensurate
with the $R^f_t$ it is divided by in B.25; the two objects would otherwise agree
only to first order in $\theta_t$. The spread B.28 follows from B.25 as
$1/Q^b_t-R^f_t=R^f_t(1-H^b_t)/H^b_t$, with the stated approximation dropping the
factor $R^f_t/H^b_t\approx1$.

\subsection{Public Authority and Market Clearing}
The government budget constraint A.4.1 is stated in real terms with a discount
bond, so only the trend deflation remains. Maturing debt has face value $B^b_{t-1}$
and pays $(1-x_t\Delta^b)$ per unit; new issuance $B^b_t$ raises $Q^b_tB^b_t$.
Deflating by $z_t$, with $b^b_t\equiv B^b_t/z_t$, the maturing stock was deflated
by $z_{t-1}$ and therefore picks up $\Gamma_t^{-1}$ while current issuance does not:
%
\begin{align}
T_t + Q^b_t\,b^b_t
&= \bigl(1-x_t\Delta^b\bigr)\,\Gamma_t^{-1}\,b^b_{t-1}
\tag{B.29}\\[6pt]
%
\mathbb{E}_t\!\left[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\,\frac{1+r_t}{1+\pi_{t+1}}\right] &= 1
\tag{B.30}\\[6pt]
%
r_t &= \rho_r\, r_{t-1}
     + (1-\rho_r)\!\left[\varphi_\pi(\pi_t - \bar{\pi})
     + \varphi_Y\!\left(y_t - \bar{y}\right) + \bar{r}\right]
\tag{B.31}\\[6pt]
%
% A.5.1  Goods market clearing
y_t &= c_t + i_t
\tag{B.32}
\label{eq:stat_closing}
\end{align}
%
Overall, the stationarised system consists of equations \eqref{eq:HHO_Deposit_Euler}, \eqref{eq:riskfree_rate} and \eqref{eq:B_capital}--\eqref{eq:stat_closing}.

% ============================================================
%  APPENDIX C — NON-STOCHASTIC STEADY STATE
%  Derived from the stationarised system of Appendix B
%  (Master_paper_Status_Quo — July 25th, evening v.3).
%
%  Conventions used below:
%    - overbars denote steady-state values of the DETRENDED variables
%      of Appendix B;
%    - the banker value-function coefficients of A.3.2.2 are renamed
%      \nu^b, \eta^b, \Omega^b to avoid the collisions with the
%      Dixit–Stiglitz elasticity \nu (A.2.1), the utilisation
%      curvature \eta (A.3.1.1) and the price-dispersion index
%      \Omega_t (A.2.13).  See the notation remark in C.0.
% ============================================================

\section{Non-Stochastic Steady State}\label{sec:SteadyState}

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Definition and Conventions}\label{sec:D0}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

The steady state of the stationarised system of
Appendix~\ref{sec:Stationarization} is the fixed point at which every
detrended variable is constant and every innovation is set to zero.
Three conventions define it precisely and are used without further
comment throughout.

\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{The disaster probability is at its mean, the disaster is not
      realised.}  We set $\theta_t=\bar\theta>0$ and $x_t=0$ for all $t$.
      This is the standard convention in the \textcite{Gourio2012}
      class of models: the indicator $x_{t+1}$ has been integrated out
      of every conditional expectation by~\eqref{eq:gourio_star}, so
      $\bar\theta$ survives inside expectations and prices risk, while
      the realised path is disaster-free.  Consequently $\bar\theta$
      enters the steady state \emph{only} through expectation
      operators; realised returns are evaluated at $x=0$.  Wherever
      the distinction matters --- the bond return \eqref{eq:A3_Rb} and
      the return on capital \eqref{eq:A3_RK} --- we write
      $\bar R^{b}$ and $\bar R^{K}$ for the realised ($x=0$) objects
      and $\mathbb{E}[\bar R^{b}]$, $\mathbb{E}[\bar R^{K}]$ for their
      conditional means.
\item \emph{The productivity innovation is off}, $\varepsilon_{z,t}=0$,
      as in the baseline of Section~2.1.  The
      gross growth factor is therefore
      $\bar\Gamma=e^{\mu}$.
\item \emph{Utilisation is normalised}, $\bar u=1$, so that
      $\bar\delta=\delta_0$ is the steady-state depreciation rate.  The
      utilisation curvature $\eta$ is then a calibration residual
      rather than a free parameter (Section~\ref{sec:D5}).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent It is convenient to record three moments of the disaster
indicator, all of them instances of~\eqref{eq:gourio_star} evaluated at
different exponents $\kappa$:
%
\begin{align}
  \mathcal{D}(\bar\theta)
    &\;\equiv\;(1-\bar\theta)+\bar\theta(1-\Delta^k)^{1-\gamma}
    &&(\kappa=1-\gamma)
    \tag{C.1a}\label{eq:C_Dcal}\\[2pt]
  \mathcal{E}(\bar\theta)
    &\;\equiv\;(1-\bar\theta)+\bar\theta(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}
     \;=\;1+\bar\theta\bigl(\Lambda^M-1\bigr)
    &&(\kappa=-\gamma)
    \tag{C.1b}\label{eq:C_Ecal}\\[2pt]
  \mathcal{K}(\bar\theta)
    &\;\equiv\;(1-\bar\theta)+\bar\theta(1-\Delta^k)
     \;=\;1-\bar\theta\Delta^k
    &&(\kappa=1)
    \tag{C.1c}\label{eq:C_Kcal}
\end{align}
%
with $\Lambda^M=(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}$ from~\eqref{eq:A3_LambdaM}. $\mathcal{D}$ is the moment that generates the pseudo-discount factor \eqref{eq:betatheta}; $\mathcal{E}$ is the moment carried by the stochastic discount factor itself and is also the normalisation  carried by the sovereign bond's resilience, $\tilde\Lambda^M=\Lambda^M/\mathcal{E}$ (equation~\eqref{eq:A3_LambdaMtilde}); it therefore enters the steady state twice, once through $\bar R^{f}$ and once through $\bar H^{b}$. $\mathcal{K}$ is the moment carried by the physical capital stock.  All three collapse to unity when $\Delta^k=0$ or $\bar\theta=0$, which is the formal sense in which the model nests \textcite{Isore2017} without capital destruction.  Keeping them distinct is essential: $\mathcal{D}$, $\mathcal{E}$ and $\mathcal{K}$ appear in different equations and substituting one for another mis-prices the risk-free rate.

\paragraph{Notation.}  Three symbols are used twice in
Appendix~\ref{AppendixA} and must be disambiguated before the bank
block can be solved.  Throughout this appendix the banker
value-function coefficients of~\eqref{eq:Banker_Value} are written
$\nu^b_t$ (marginal value of net worth), $\eta^b_t$ (marginal value of
assets) and $\Omega^b_{t+1}$ (survival-weighted continuation value), so
that $\nu$ remains the Dixit--Stiglitz elasticity of~\eqref{eq:aggregator},
$\eta$ the utilisation curvature of~\eqref{eq:A3_util}, and $\Omega_t$
the price-dispersion index of~(A.2.13).

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Preferences, Discounting and the Risk-Free Rate}\label{sec:D2}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

\paragraph{Pseudo-discount factor.}
Evaluating~\eqref{eq:betatheta} at $\theta_t=\bar\theta$,
%
\begin{equation}
  \beta(\bar\theta)\;=\;\beta_0\,\mathcal{D}(\bar\theta)^{\frac{1-\psi}{1-\gamma}}
  \tag{C.2}\label{eq:C_betabar}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Stochastic discount factor.}
Write the detrended SDF~\eqref{eq:B_SDF} in the equivalent form
obtained by substituting $\chi(1-\psi)=\gamma-\psi$,
%
\begin{equation*}
  \mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}
  \;=\;\beta_0
  \left(\frac{c_{t+1}}{c_t}\right)^{\!-\psi}
  \left(\frac{1-L_{t+1}}{1-L_t}\right)^{\!\varpi(1-\psi)}
  \left(\frac{v_{t+1}\Gamma_{t+1}}
             {\bigl(\mathbb{E}_t\bigl[(v_{t+1}\Gamma_{t+1})^{1-\gamma}\bigr]\bigr)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}}
  \right)^{\!\psi-\gamma}
  \Gamma_{t+1}^{-\psi}
\end{equation*}
%
The two forms are the same object. Collecting the trend factors,
$\bigl(v_{t+1}\Gamma_{t+1}\bigr)^{\psi-\gamma}\Gamma_{t+1}^{-\psi}
 = v_{t+1}^{\psi-\gamma}\,\Gamma_{t+1}^{-\gamma}$,
which is the $\Gamma_{t+1}^{-\gamma}$ loading of~\eqref{eq:B_SDF}; and
$v_{t+1}^{\psi-\gamma}=v_{t+1}^{-\chi(1-\psi)}$ by the same substitution. The
form written here simply groups the trend with the value function instead of
separating them, which is what makes the steady-state evaluation below a
one-line calculation.  At
the steady state $c_{t+1}=c_t$, $L_{t+1}=L_t$ and $v_{t+1}=\bar v$, so
the two growth-free ratios equal unity and, with $\varepsilon_z=0$,
$\Gamma_{t+1}=e^{\mu}(1-\Delta^k)^{x_{t+1}}$.  Then
$\bigl(\mathbb{E}_t[(\bar v\Gamma_{t+1})^{1-\gamma}]\bigr)^{1/(1-\gamma)}
 =\bar v\,e^{\mu}\mathcal{D}^{1/(1-\gamma)}$
by~\eqref{eq:gourio_star}, and the SDF in the disaster-free realisation
$x_{t+1}=0$ is
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}
  \;=\;\beta_0\,e^{-\psi\mu}\,\mathcal{D}^{\frac{\gamma-\psi}{1-\gamma}}
  \;=\;\frac{\beta(\bar\theta)\,e^{-\psi\mu}}{\mathcal{D}}
  \tag{C.3}\label{eq:C_Qf}
\end{equation}
%
while in the disaster realisation $x_{t+1}=1$ every occurrence of
$\Gamma_{t+1}$ contributes an extra factor $(1-\Delta^k)$, so that
$\bar Q^{d}/\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}=(1-\Delta^k)^{-\psi}(1-\Delta^k)^{\psi-\gamma}
 =(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}=\Lambda^M$.
This reproduces the closed form~\eqref{eq:A3_LambdaM} from the
stationarised SDF rather than from the level-form argument of
Section~\ref{par:asset_specific_resilience}, and is the internal
consistency check that the two derivations agree.

\paragraph{Risk-free and deposit rates.}
Taking expectations, $\mathbb{E}[\bar Q]=\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}\mathcal{E}$, so
\eqref{eq:riskfree_rate} gives
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar R^{f}
  \;=\;\frac{1}{\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}\,\mathcal{E}}
  \;=\;\frac{e^{\psi\mu}}{\beta_0\,
       \mathcal{D}^{\frac{\gamma-\psi}{1-\gamma}}\,\mathcal{E}}
  \;=\;\frac{\mathcal{D}\,e^{\psi\mu}}{\beta(\bar\theta)\,\mathcal{E}}\,,
  \qquad
  \bar R^{d}=\bar R^{f}
  \tag{C.4}\label{eq:C_Rf}
\end{equation}
%
where $\bar R^{d}=\bar R^{f}$ is deposit-market clearing
(Section~\ref{appsec:households}).  At $\Delta^k=0$ or $\bar\theta=0$ we
have $\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{E}=1$ and~\eqref{eq:C_Rf} collapses to
$\bar R^{f}=e^{\psi\mu}/\beta_0$, the risk-free rate of the
disaster-free growth model.  The two moments enter with opposite signs:
$\mathcal{E}>1$ is the precautionary term that lowers the risk-free
rate as $\bar\theta$ rises, $\mathcal{D}>1$ (for $\gamma>1$) is the
pseudo-discounting term that partly offsets it.

\paragraph{Growth-adjusted discount factor.}
Several forward-looking equations (the Bellman
recursion~\eqref{eq:B_bellman} and the Calvo
auxiliaries~\eqref{eq:B_Xi1}--\eqref{eq:B_Xi2}) discount a variable that
grows with the trend, and therefore carry the product
$\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}\Gamma_{t+1}$ rather than $\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}$ alone.  Because the SDF
and the growth factor are both functions of the same indicator
$x_{t+1}$, the expectation of the product is \emph{not} the product of
the expectations.  Applying~\eqref{eq:gourio_star} at
$\kappa=(-\gamma)+1=1-\gamma$ to the combined exponent,
%
\begin{equation}
  \Theta\;\equiv\;\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bar Q\,\bar\Gamma\bigr]
  \;=\;\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}e^{\mu}\mathcal{D}
  \;=\;\beta(\bar\theta)\,e^{(1-\psi)\mu}
  \;<\;\mathbb{E}[\bar Q]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\bar\Gamma]
       \;=\;\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}e^{\mu}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{K}
  \tag{C.5}\label{eq:C_Theta}
\end{equation}
%
the inequality holding whenever $\gamma>0$.  The object that discounts
trending payoffs is thus exactly $\beta(\bar\theta)e^{(1-\psi)\mu}$,
and this --- not $\bar R^{f}$ --- is the natural place at which
$\beta(\bar\theta)$ appears in closed form.

\paragraph{Value function.}
Evaluating the Bellman recursion~\eqref{eq:B_bellman} at the steady
state, the certainty equivalent is
$\mathcal{R}^{v}=\bar v^{1-\psi}e^{(1-\psi)\mu}
 \mathcal{D}^{(1-\psi)/(1-\gamma)}$, so that
$\beta_0\mathcal{R}^{v}=\Theta\,\bar v^{1-\psi}$ and
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar v^{1-\psi}\bigl[1-\Theta\bigr]
  =\bigl[\bar c\,(1-\bar L)^{\varpi}\bigr]^{1-\psi}
  \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad
  \bar v=\frac{\bar c\,(1-\bar L)^{\varpi}}
              {\bigl(1-\Theta\bigr)^{\frac{1}{1-\psi}}}
  \tag{C.6}\label{eq:C_v}
\end{equation}
%
which requires $\Theta<1$, i.e.\ $\beta(\bar\theta)e^{(1-\psi)\mu}<1$.
For $\psi>1$ this is weaker than $\beta_0<1$ and is satisfied for all
admissible calibrations; it is nonetheless the binding existence
condition for the household block and should be verified numerically.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Sovereign Bond Block}\label{sec:D3}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

From~\eqref{eq:A3_resil} and~\eqref{eq:A3_bondprice},
%
\begin{align}
 \bar H^{b} &= 1-\bar\theta\,\Delta^b\,\tilde\Lambda^M,
  \qquad
  \tilde\Lambda^M=\frac{(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}}{\mathcal{E}(\bar\theta)}
  \tag{C.7a}\label{eq:C_Hb}\\[2pt]
  \bar Q^{b} &= \frac{\bar H^{b}}{\bar R^{f}} .
  \tag{C.7b}\label{eq:C_Qb}
\end{align}
%
The realised gross bond return follows from~\eqref{eq:A3_Rb} at $x=0$,
and its conditional mean from~\eqref{eq:A3_ERb}:
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar R^{b}\;=\;\frac{1}{\bar Q^{b}}\;=\;\frac{\bar R^{f}}{\bar H^{b}}
  \;>\;\bar R^{f},
  \qquad
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[\bar R^{b}\bigr]
  =\frac{1-\bar\theta\Delta^b}{\bar Q^{b}}
  =\frac{\bigl(1-\bar\theta\Delta^b\bigr)\bar R^{f}}{\bar H^{b}} .
  \tag{C.8}\label{eq:C_Rb}
\end{equation}
%
The strict inequality is not an arbitrage violation: along the
disaster-free path banks collect the sovereign risk premium every
period without ever bearing the haircut, which is exactly what the
non-stochastic steady state of a rare-disaster model represents.  It
does mean that $\bar R^{b}$, not $\mathbb{E}[\bar R^{b}]$, is the object
that enters the bank net-worth accumulation~\eqref{eq:Bank_Net_worth} at
the steady state, since that equation is stated in realised returns.
The promised-yield spread~\eqref{eq:A3_spread} is
%
\begin{equation}
 \overline{\mathrm{spread}}
  =\frac{1}{\bar Q^{b}}-\bar R^{f}
  =\bar R^{f}\,\frac{\bar\theta\,\Delta^b\,\tilde\Lambda^M}{\bar H^{b}}
  \tag{C.9}\label{eq:C_spread}
\end{equation}
%
which is increasing in $\bar\theta$, in $\Delta^b$ and --- through
$\Lambda^M=(1-\Delta^k)^{-\gamma}$ --- in both risk aversion and the
size of the capital disaster.  The last dependence is the sense in
which the sovereign spread in this model is a genuine \emph{risk}
premium rather than a pure expected-loss compensation: with $\gamma=0$
the spread would collapse to $\bar R^{f}\bar\theta\Delta^b/\bar H^b$.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Price Setting}\label{sec:D4}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

Steady-state inflation $\bar\pi$ is a policy parameter.  Inverting the
aggregate inflation condition~\eqref{eq:B_agg_pi},
%
\begin{equation}
  1+\bar\pi^{*}
  =\left[\frac{(1+\bar\pi)^{1-\nu}-\zeta}{1-\zeta}\right]^{\frac{1}{1-\nu}} ,
  \tag{C.10}\label{eq:C_pistar}
\end{equation}
%
and solving the dispersion recursion~\eqref{eq:B_dispersion} for its
fixed point,
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar\Omega
  =\frac{(1-\zeta)\Bigl(\dfrac{1+\bar\pi}{1+\bar\pi^{*}}\Bigr)^{\nu}}
        {1-\zeta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}} ,
  \tag{C.11}\label{eq:C_Omega}
\end{equation}
%
which requires $\zeta(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}<1$.  At $\bar\pi=0$ both reduce
to $\bar\pi^{*}=0$ and $\bar\Omega=1$.

The Calvo auxiliaries~\eqref{eq:B_Xi1}--\eqref{eq:B_Xi2} discount a
trending payoff and therefore carry $\Theta$ from~\eqref{eq:C_Theta}:
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar\xi_{1}=\frac{\bar y\,\overline{mc}}{1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}},
  \qquad
  \bar\xi_{2}=\frac{\bar y}{1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu-1}} ,
  \tag{C.12}\label{eq:C_xi}
\end{equation}
%
both requiring $\zeta\Theta(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}<1$.  Substituting into the reset-price condition~\eqref{eq:B_reset_pi} and solving for
marginal cost,
%
their ratio is
%
\begin{equation*}
  \frac{\bar\xi_1}{\bar\xi_2}
  =\overline{mc}\;
   \frac{1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu-1}}
        {1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}} ,
\end{equation*}
%
so that~\eqref{eq:B_reset_pi} evaluated at the steady state reads
$(1+\bar\pi^{*})=\frac{\nu}{\nu-1}(\bar\xi_1/\bar\xi_2)(1+\bar\pi)$ and
inverts to

\begin{equation}
  \overline{mc}
  =\frac{\nu-1}{\nu}\,
   \frac{1+\bar\pi^{*}}{1+\bar\pi}\;
   \frac{1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}}
        {1-\zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu-1}} .
  \tag{C.13}\label{eq:C_mc}
\end{equation}
%
At $\bar\pi=0$ this is the familiar $\overline{mc}=(\nu-1)/\nu$: the
inverse of the flexible-price mark-up.  Positive trend inflation raises
$\overline{mc}$ above that level.  Finally, the Fisher
equation~\eqref{eq:A4_fisher} pins the steady-state nominal rate,
%
\begin{equation}
  1+\bar r=\bar R^{f}\,(1+\bar\pi) ,
  \tag{C.14}\label{eq:C_r}
\end{equation}
%
at which the Taylor rule~\eqref{eq:A4_taylor} is satisfied identically. The
exact no-arbitrage form~\eqref{eq:A4_fisher} and the textbook approximation
$\bar R^{f}=(1+\bar r)/\mathbb{E}[1+\bar\pi]$ coincide here, because at the
non-stochastic steady state $\pi_{t+1}=\bar\pi$ is deterministic: both the
covariance term and the Jensen gap that separate them
(paragraph~\ref{par:A17}) vanish identically. The distinction between the two
is therefore invisible in this appendix and appears only in the dynamics, at
second order and above.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Capital, Utilisation and the Return on Capital}\label{sec:D5}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

With $\bar i/\bar k=\bar I/\bar K$ the adjustment cost and its
derivative vanish, so Tobin's~$Q$~\eqref{eq:B_TobinsQ} gives
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}=1,
  \qquad
  \overline{\Pi^{cp}}=0 ,
  \tag{C.15}\label{eq:C_Qtob}
\end{equation}
%
confirming the zero-profit property of the capital-goods producer
asserted in Section~\ref{par:CP}.  The capital law of
motion~\eqref{eq:B_capital} at $\bar u=1$ and $\mathcal{S}(\bar i/\bar k)\bar k=\bar i$
gives
%
\begin{equation}
  e^{\mu}\bar k=(1-\delta_0)\bar k+\bar i
  \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad
  \bar i=\bigl(e^{\mu}-1+\delta_0\bigr)\bar k ,
  \tag{C.16}\label{eq:C_i}
\end{equation}
%
and effective capital is $\bar{\tilde k}=\bar u\bar k=\bar k$
by~\eqref{eq:B_Ktilde}.  The two dating conventions for the capital
stock are related by
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar k^{n}=e^{\mu}\,\bar k ,
  \tag{C.17}\label{eq:C_kn}
\end{equation}
%
since $k^{n}_{t+1}=K_{t+1}/z_t$ and $k_{t+1}=K_{t+1}/z_{t+1}$.  This
factor matters: $\bar k^{n}$ is the object that appears in the
entrepreneur balance sheet~\eqref{eq:A3_entrep_BS} (in detrended form, B.20) and in the equity
ratio of the external finance premium, whereas $\bar k$ is the object
that appears in the production function.

Evaluated at $\bar u=1$ and $\bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}=1$, the disaster-free
component of the return on capital~\eqref{eq:A3_RK} (in detrended form, B.16) is
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar{\tilde R}^{K}=\bar r_t^k+1-\delta_0 ,
  \qquad
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[\bar R^{K}\bigr]=\mathcal{K}(\bar\theta)\,\bar{\tilde R}^{K}
  =\bigl(1-\bar\theta\Delta^k\bigr)\bar{\tilde R}^{K} ,
  \tag{C.18}\label{eq:C_RK}
\end{equation}
%
with $\bar R^{K}=\bar{\tilde R}^{K}$ the realised return along the
disaster-free path.  Finally, the utilisation
condition~\eqref{eq:A3_util} at $\bar u=1$ reads
$\bar r^k_t=\delta_0\eta$, which under the
normalisation of Section~\ref{sec:D0} pins the curvature parameter
%
\begin{equation}
  \eta=\frac{\bar r^k_t}{\delta_0} .
  \tag{C.19}\label{eq:C_eta}
\end{equation}
%
The second-order condition $\eta>1$ therefore imposes the admissibility
restriction $\bar r^k_t>\delta_0$ on the calibration: the
steady-state rental rate must exceed the depreciation rate, which is
the requirement that operating capital at full utilisation is
profitable at the margin.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Production and Aggregate Quantities}\label{sec:D6}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

The factor-price conditions~(B.6)--\eqref{eq:B_rental}
depend on capital and labour only through the ratio
$\bar{\tilde k}/\bar L$.  Inverting the rental-rate condition,
%
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\bar{\tilde k}}{\bar L}
  =\left(\frac{\overline{mc}\,\alpha}{\bar  r^k_t}\right)^{\!\frac{1}{1-\alpha}},
  \qquad
  \bar w=\overline{mc}\,(1-\alpha)
        \left(\frac{\bar{\tilde k}}{\bar L}\right)^{\!\alpha}.
  \tag{C.20}\label{eq:C_kl}
\end{equation}
%
The production function~\eqref{eq:B_production} and the
investment relation~\eqref{eq:C_i} are both homogeneous of degree one
in $(\bar k,\bar L)$, so output, investment and --- by goods-market
clearing~\eqref{eq:stat_closing} --- consumption are all pinned per unit
of labour:
%
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\bar y}{\bar L}
  =\frac{1}{\bar\Omega}\left(\frac{\bar{\tilde k}}{\bar L}\right)^{\!\alpha},
  \qquad
  \frac{\bar i}{\bar L}
  =\bigl(e^{\mu}-1+\delta_0\bigr)\frac{\bar{\tilde k}}{\bar L},
  \qquad
  \frac{\bar c}{\bar L}=\frac{\bar y}{\bar L}-\frac{\bar i}{\bar L} .
  \tag{C.21}\label{eq:C_perL}
\end{equation}
%
The scale is then closed by the labour--leisure
condition~\eqref{eq:B_HHO_C}, $\varpi\bar c=\bar w(1-\bar L)$.  Dividing
by $\bar L$ and rearranging gives the closed form
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar L=\frac{1}{1+\dfrac{\varpi}{\bar w}\cdot\dfrac{\bar c}{\bar L}} ,
  \tag{C.22}\label{eq:C_L}
\end{equation}
%
after which $\bar k=(\bar{\tilde k}/\bar L)\bar L$,
$\bar y=(\bar y/\bar L)\bar L$, $\bar c$ and $\bar i$ follow.  Equation
\eqref{eq:C_L} treats the leisure weight $\varpi$ as a deep parameter
and lets hours be determined; the alternative convention --- targeting
$\bar L$ and inverting~\eqref{eq:C_L} for $\varpi$ --- is discussed in
the calibration section.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Entrepreneur Block}\label{sec:D7}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

Let
%
\begin{equation}
  \varkappa\;\equiv\;\frac{\bar n^{e}}{\bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}\bar k^{n}}
  \;=\;\frac{\bar n^{e}}{e^{\mu}\bar k}
  \tag{C.23}\label{eq:C_kappa}
\end{equation}
%
denote the steady-state entrepreneur equity ratio, the argument of the
external finance premium~\eqref{eq:A3_EFP}.  The free-entry
condition~\eqref{eq:A3_indiff} evaluated at the steady state is
%
\begin{equation}
  \mathbb{E}\bigl[\bar R^{K}\bigr]
  \;=\;f_0\,\varkappa^{-\chi^{e}}\,\bar R^{S}
  \;\equiv\;\mathcal{F}\,\bar R^{S},
  \qquad
  \mathcal{F}\equiv f_0\varkappa^{-\chi^{e}} ,
  \tag{C.24}\label{eq:C_partic}
\end{equation}
%
where $\mathcal{F}>1$ is the gross external finance premium.  Combining
\eqref{eq:C_partic} with~\eqref{eq:C_RK} delivers the rental rate as a
function of the loan rate alone,
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar  r^k_t
  =\frac{\mathcal{F}\,\bar R^{S}}{1-\bar\theta\Delta^k}-(1-\delta_0) ,
  \tag{C.25}\label{eq:C_pkr}
\end{equation}
%
which is the equation that replaces \textcite{Isore2017}'s household
capital Euler $\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}R^{K}_{t+1}]=1$.  Two wedges separate
$\bar r^k_t$ from its \textcite{Isore2017} counterpart:
the entrepreneur premium $\mathcal{F}$ and the bank margin implicit in
$\bar R^{S}>\bar R^{d}=\bar R^{f}$.  Setting
$\mathcal{F}=1$ and $\bar R^{S}=\bar R^{f}$ recovers their steady state
exactly.

The entrepreneur balance sheet~\eqref{eq:A3_entrep_BS} (in detrended form, B.20) gives loan demand
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}\bar s
  =\bar k^{n}-\bar n^{e}
  =(1-\varkappa)\,e^{\mu}\bar k ,
  \tag{C.26}\label{eq:C_QS}
\end{equation}
%
and net-worth accumulation~\eqref{eq:A3_NE} (in detrended form, B.19) --- in which every bracketed
term is dated $t-1$ and therefore carries $\bar\Gamma^{-1}=e^{-\mu}$ ---
becomes
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar n^{e}
  =\sigma^{e}\Bigl[\bigl(\bar{\tilde R}^{K}-\bar R^{S}\bigr)\bar k
    +\bar R^{S}e^{-\mu}\bar n^{e}\Bigr]+\iota^{e} ,
  \tag{C.27}\label{eq:C_ne}
\end{equation}
%
using $e^{-\mu}\bar k^{n}=\bar k$.  Solving,
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar n^{e}
  =\frac{\sigma^{e}\bigl(\bar{\tilde R}^{K}-\bar R^{S}\bigr)\bar k+\iota^{e}}
        {1-\sigma^{e}e^{-\mu}\bar R^{S}} ,
  \qquad\text{equivalently}\qquad
  \iota^{e}
  =\bar n^{e}\bigl(1-\sigma^{e}e^{-\mu}\bar R^{S}\bigr)
   -\sigma^{e}\bigl(\bar{\tilde R}^{K}-\bar R^{S}\bigr)\bar k .
  \tag{C.28}\label{eq:C_iotae}
\end{equation}
%
Existence of a positive, finite $\bar n^{e}$ requires
$\sigma^{e}e^{-\mu}\bar R^{S}<1$, i.e.\ the entrepreneur survival rate
must be low enough relative to the growth-adjusted loan rate for net
worth not to grow without bound.  In practice the second form is used:
$\varkappa$ is the calibration target and $\iota^{e}$ the residual
transfer that supports it.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Bank Block}\label{sec:D8}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

\paragraph{Balance sheet.}
The home-bias identity~\eqref{eq:A3_homebias} together with the binding
leverage constraint~\eqref{eq:A323} gives the entire asset side as a
multiple of bank net worth,
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar A=\frac{\bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}\bar s}{1-\phi}=\lambda\,\bar n^{b},
  \qquad
  \bar Q^{b}\bar b^{b}=\phi\lambda\bar n^{b},
  \qquad
  \bar d=(\lambda-1)\,\bar n^{b} ,
  \tag{C.29}\label{eq:C_bankBS}
\end{equation}
%
so that, combining with~\eqref{eq:C_QS}, loan-market
clearing~(A.5.4) determines bank net worth residually:
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar n^{b}=\frac{(1-\varkappa)\,e^{\mu}\bar k}{(1-\phi)\,\lambda} .
  \tag{C.30}\label{eq:C_nb}
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Net-worth accumulation and the start-up transfer.}
Substituting the portfolio shares
$\bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}\bar s=(1-\phi)\lambda\bar n^{b}$ and
$\bar Q^{b}\bar b^{b}=\phi\lambda\bar n^{b}$
into~\eqref{eq:Bank_Net_worth}, and using the \emph{realised} bond return
$\bar R^{b}=1/\bar Q^{b}$ of~\eqref{eq:C_Rb},
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar n^{b}
  =\sigma^{b}e^{-\mu}
    \Bigl\{\lambda\bigl[(1-\phi)(\bar R^{S}-\bar R^{f})
          +\phi(\bar R^{b}-\bar R^{f})\bigr]+\bar R^{f}\Bigr\}\bar n^{b}
   +\iota^b ,
  \tag{C.31}\label{eq:C_nbLOM}
\end{equation}
%
hence
%
\begin{equation}
  \iota=\bar n^{b}
  \Bigl(1-\sigma^{b}e^{-\mu}
    \bigl\{\lambda\bigl[(1-\phi)(\bar R^{S}-\bar R^{f})
          +\phi(\bar R^{b}-\bar R^{f})\bigr]+\bar R^{f}\bigr\}\Bigr) .
  \tag{C.32}\label{eq:C_iota}
\end{equation}
%
The bracketed object is the gross return on the bank's portfolio net of funding costs, levered by $\lambda$; $\iota>0$ requires it to be smaller than $e^{\mu}/\sigma^{b}$, the growth-adjusted survival-corrected upper bound beyond which bank net worth would grow without limit and the leverage constraint would eventually cease to bind.

\paragraph{Value-function coefficients and the diversion parameter.}
Appendix~\ref{par:A322} derives the recursions for $\nu^b_t,\eta^b_t,\Omega_t$ but defers the mapping to the underlying moral-hazard parameter $\theta^b$; that mapping is completed here. Write the asset-weighted excess return using the portfolio shares,
%
\begin{equation*}
  \bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}_{t+1} = (1-\phi)\bigl(R^S_t-R^d_t\bigr) + \phi\bigl(R^b_{t+1}-R^d_t\bigr)  \tag{C.33}
\end{equation*}
%
Both $\nu_t$ and $\eta_t$ discount using the \emph{realised} (no-default, $x=0$) bond return $R^b_{t+1}=1/Q^b_t$, not the fully theta-weighted conditional expectation of the bond payoff. This is not a further approximation layered on top of the value-function derivation of Appendix~\ref{par:A322}: it is the same convention already used, without comment, by the net-worth accumulation equation~\eqref{eq:Bank_Net_worth} two paragraphs above, and by every other equation in this appendix in which $R^b_t$ appears. The two objects are not interchangeable in general --- $\mathbb{E}_t[R^b_{t+1}]=(1-\theta_t\Delta^b)/Q^b_t$ is strictly below the realised return $1/Q^b_t$, equation~\eqref{eq:A3_ERb} --- and the bond-pricing identity $\mathbb{E}_t[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}R^b_{t+1}]=1$, which would set the bond's contribution to $\eta_t$ to exactly zero, holds only for the former. Using it here would price the banker's continuation value against a hypothetical default branch that this appendix's own solution method never actually realises (Appendix~\ref{sec:banking_block}, the note on the absence of a realised $x_t=1$ regime): the value function must be consistent with the \emph{same} net-worth process it is the marginal value of, and that process only ever compounds the realised return. The bond's steady-state excess return over the risk-free rate is therefore not zero but exactly the steady-state sovereign spread, $\bar R^b-\bar R^f=\overline{\mathrm{spread}}$ (equation~\eqref{eq:A3_spread}), and it enters $\eta_t$ on the same footing as the loan spread. Defining
%
\begin{equation}
  \varsigma \;\equiv\; \frac{(1-\phi)(\bar R^S-\bar R^f)+\phi\,\overline{\mathrm{spread}}}{\bar R^f} ,
  \tag{C.34}\label{eq:C_varsigma}
\end{equation}
%
the coefficient recursions of Section~\ref{par:A322} collapse, exactly as before, to the recursions~\eqref{eq:B_etab}--\eqref{eq:B_lambdat}. At the steadystate $\mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathcal{M}}]=1/\bar R^{f}$ and $\bar R^{d}=\bar R^{f}$, so~\eqref{eq:B_nub} gives
%
\begin{equation*}
  \bar\nu^b=\frac{\bar\Omega^b\,\bar R^{d}}{\bar R^{f}}=\bar\Omega^b,
  \qquad
  \bar\eta^b=\frac{\bar\Omega^b\,\bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}}{\bar R^{f}}
            =\varsigma\,\bar\nu^b ,
\end{equation*}
%
the second using $\bar R^{\,\mathrm{ex}}=\varsigma\bar R^{f}$ by the definition
of $\varsigma$ in~\eqref{eq:C_varsigma}. The marginal value of net worth is
therefore the continuation value itself, and the marginal value of assets is
that same object scaled by the levered excess return. Substituting both into
$\bar\Omega^b=(1-\sigma^b)+\sigma^b(\bar\nu^b+\lambda\bar\eta^b)$ leaves a
single equation in $\bar\Omega^b$,
%
\begin{equation*}
  \bar\Omega^b=(1-\sigma^b)+\sigma^b\,\bar\Omega^b\bigl(1+\lambda\varsigma\bigr),
\end{equation*}
%
which solves to
\begin{equation}
  \bar\nu^b=\frac{1-\sigma^b}{1-\sigma^b\bigl(1+\lambda\varsigma\bigr)},
  \qquad
  \bar\eta^b=\varsigma\,\bar\nu^b,
  \qquad
  \theta^b=\bar\eta^b+\frac{\bar\nu^b}{\lambda}=\bar\nu^b\Bigl(\varsigma+\frac1\lambda\Bigr) ,
  \tag{C.35}\label{eq:C_nub}
\end{equation}
%
the last equality inverting $\lambda=\bar\nu^b/(\theta^b-\bar\eta^b)$. Equation~\eqref{eq:C_nub} is what makes the endogenous-$\lambda_t$ derivation of Section~\ref{par:A322} internally disciplined rather than free: given a target steady-state leverage $\lambda$ and the steady-state loan and bond margins, the implied diversion parameter $\theta^b$ is uniquely determined and can be compared with the values used in \textcite{Gertler2011}. The admissibility condition simplifies relative to a loan-only portfolio: $\sigma^b(1+\lambda\varsigma)<1$ is still required for $\bar\nu^b$ to be positive and finite, but $\bar\eta^b>0$ now holds for any $\phi\in[0,1]$ provided $\bar R^S>\bar R^f$ or $\overline{\mathrm{spread}}>0$ --- either wedge alone is enough, since $\varsigma$ is a weighted average of two individually positive terms rather than a single term scaled by $(1-\phi)$.

Home bias no longer dilutes franchise value at this calibration: since the promised-yield sovereign spread exceeds the bank loan spread ($\overline{\mathrm{spread}}\approx0.8504\%$ against $\bar R^S-\bar R^f\approx0.20\%$ quarterly at the baseline calibration), $\varsigma$ is \emph{increasing} in $\phi$ --- a larger sovereign bond share raises, rather than dilutes, the bank's steady-state franchise value. A higher $\phi$ still requires a higher $\theta^b$ to hold leverage at the same target $\lambda$, but for the reverse reason to before: it is precisely because a more valuable franchise is more tempting to divert that a stronger deterrent is needed to keep the constraint binding at an unchanged leverage limit.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Public Authority}\label{sec:D9}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

Using the real, discount-bond form of the government financing identity
employed in the consolidation of Section~\ref{sec:A5},
$T_t=R^{b}_tQ^{b}_{t-1}B^{b}_{t-1}-Q^{b}_tB^{b}_t$, detrended and
evaluated at the steady state with $\bar\Gamma^{-1}=e^{-\mu}$ and
$\bar b=\bar b^{b}$:
%
\begin{equation}
  \bar T
  =\bar Q^{b}\bar b\bigl(e^{-\mu}\bar R^{b}-1\bigr)
  =\bar b\,e^{-\mu}-\bar Q^{b}\bar b
  =\bar Q^{b}\bar b\left(\frac{e^{-\mu}}{\bar Q^{b}}-1\right) ,
  \tag{C.36}\label{eq:C_T}
\end{equation}
%
with the bond quantity recovered from the value already determined
in~\eqref{eq:C_bankBS}, $\bar b=\phi\lambda\bar n^{b}/\bar Q^{b}$.
Taxes are positive whenever $\bar Q^{b}<e^{-\mu}$, i.e.\ whenever the
real bond yield exceeds trend growth --- the usual $r>g$ condition for a
balanced-budget debt rollover to require primary revenue.  The Taylor
rule~\eqref{eq:A5_taylor} holds identically at $\bar r$
from~\eqref{eq:C_r} and imposes no further restriction.

% ------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Solution Algorithm and Summary}\label{sec:D10}
% ------------------------------------------------------------

The steady state admits a fully recursive closed-form solution: no
numerical fixed point is required.  

Three objects are calibration inputs rather than model outputs and are taken
as given at the start: the steady-state bank leverage $\lambda$, the bank
margin $\bar R^{S}/\bar R^{d}$, and the entrepreneur equity ratio $\varkappa$.
Each is matched to a data target rather than solved for, and each has a
structural parameter that is then recovered residually --- the diversion
fraction $\theta^{b}$ from $\lambda$ in step~7, the premium scale $f_0$ from
$\varkappa$ in step~6. This inversion is what keeps the calibration disciplined:
the free parameters are the ones that are hard to observe, and the ones matched
directly are the ones that are not.

The ordering is

\begin{enumerate}\itemsep2pt
\item \textbf{Disaster moments and discounting.}
      $\Lambda^M$; $\mathcal{D},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{K}$
      \eqref{eq:C_Dcal}--\eqref{eq:C_Kcal}; $\beta(\bar\theta)$
      \eqref{eq:C_betabar}; $\bar \mathcal{M}^(f)_{t,t+1}$ \eqref{eq:C_Qf};
      $\bar R^{f}=\bar R^{d}$ \eqref{eq:C_Rf}; $\Theta$
      \eqref{eq:C_Theta}.
\item \textbf{Sovereign block.} $\bar H^{b},\bar Q^{b},\bar R^{b},
      \mathbb{E}[\bar R^{b}],\overline{\mathrm{spread}}$
      \eqref{eq:C_Hb}--\eqref{eq:C_spread}.
\item \textbf{Nominal block.} $\bar\pi^{*},\bar\Omega,\overline{mc},\bar r$
      \eqref{eq:C_pistar}--\eqref{eq:C_r}.
\item \textbf{Rates and capital return.} $\bar R^{S}$ from the target
      bank margin; $\mathcal{F}$ from the target entrepreneur premium;
      $\bar  r^k_t$ \eqref{eq:C_pkr}; $\bar{\tilde R}^{K}$
      \eqref{eq:C_RK}; $\eta$ \eqref{eq:C_eta}.
\item \textbf{Real block.} $\bar{\tilde k}/\bar L,\bar w$
      \eqref{eq:C_kl}; per-labour ratios \eqref{eq:C_perL};
      $\bar L$ \eqref{eq:C_L}; then $\bar k,\bar y,\bar c,\bar i,
      \bar{\tilde k},\bar\xi_1,\bar\xi_2,\bar v$.
\item \textbf{Financial quantities.} $\bar k^{n}$ \eqref{eq:C_kn};
      $\bar n^{e}=\varkappa\bar k^{n}$; $f_0=\mathcal{F}\varkappa^{\chi^{e}}$;
      $\bar Q^{\mathrm{tob}}\bar s$ \eqref{eq:C_QS};
      $\bar n^{b}$ \eqref{eq:C_nb}; $\bar Q^{b}\bar b^{b},\bar d$
      \eqref{eq:C_bankBS}; $\iota^{e}$ \eqref{eq:C_iotae};
      $\iota$ \eqref{eq:C_iota}.
\item \textbf{Bank value function.} $\varsigma,\bar\nu^{b},\bar\eta^{b},
      \theta^{b}$ \eqref{eq:C_varsigma}--\eqref{eq:C_nub}.
\item \textbf{Fiscal residual.} $\bar b,\bar T$ \eqref{eq:C_T}.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent Steps 1--3 are entirely independent of the banking block and
reproduce \textcite{Isore2017} whenever $\Delta^k$ and $\bar\theta$
enter; steps 4--7 are where the two financial wedges $\mathcal{F}$ and
$\bar R^{S}/\bar R^{d}$ displace their capital Euler.

\paragraph{Admissibility conditions.}
The calibration must satisfy
%
\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{lll}
  \text{(i)} & \Theta=\beta(\bar\theta)e^{(1-\psi)\mu}<1
             & \text{finite household value function \eqref{eq:C_v}}\\[2pt]
  \text{(ii)} & \zeta\,\Theta\,(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}<1
              \;\text{ and }\;\zeta(1+\bar\pi)^{\nu}<1
             & \text{convergent Calvo sums \eqref{eq:C_xi}, \eqref{eq:C_Omega}}\\[2pt]
  \text{(iii)} & \bar  r^k_t>\delta_0
             & \text{utilisation SOC, }\eta>1\text{ \eqref{eq:C_eta}}\\[2pt]
  \text{(iv)} & \sigma^{e}e^{-\mu}\bar R^{S}<1
             & \text{bounded entrepreneur net worth \eqref{eq:C_iotae}}\\[2pt]
  \text{(v)} & \sigma^{b}\bigl(1+\lambda\varsigma\bigr)<1
             & \text{bounded banker franchise value \eqref{eq:C_nub}}\\[2pt]
  \text{(vi)} & \varsigma>0,\;\text{i.e. }\bar R^{S}>\bar R^{f}
              \;\text{ or }\;\overline{\mathrm{spread}}>0
             & \bar\eta^{b}>0,\text{ leverage constraint binds}\\[2pt]
  \text{(vii)} & \iota^b>0,\;\iota^{e}>0
             & \text{positive start-up transfers \eqref{eq:C_iota}, \eqref{eq:C_iotae}}
\end{array}
\end{equation*}

\paragraph{Nesting.}
Two limits are worth recording explicitly.  At $\phi=0$ the sovereign bond disappears from the bank balance sheet: $\bar Q^{b}\bar b^{b}=0$, $\varsigma=(\bar R^{S}-\bar R^{f})/\bar R^{f}$, and~\eqref{eq:C_nbLOM} loses its bond term, so that the counterfactual steady state differs from the baseline only through the bank's portfolio composition and the implied $\theta^{b}$ --- not through any of the real aggregates, since $\bar R^{b}$ enters no other equation.  At $\bar\theta=0$ or $\Delta^k=0$ we have $\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{K}=1$, $\Lambda^M=1$, $\bar R^{f}=e^{\psi\mu}/\beta_0$, $\overline{\mathrm{spread}} =\bar R^{f}\,\bar\theta\Delta^b/(1-\bar\theta\Delta^b)$, since $\tilde\Lambda^M\to1$ together with $\mathcal{E}\to1$, and the whole disaster apparatus collapses; the residual banking block is then a standard \textcite{Gertler2011}--\textcite{Bernanke1999} steady state.

\section{Long-term bond structure}\label{sec:LongTermBond}

% ============================================================
% Long-duration bond derivation, complete
% ============================================================

The following long-term bond structure was derived against \textcite{Gabaix2012}, \textcite{Woodford2001}-style perpetuity bonds, and \textcite{Bocola2016}'s own use of long-term government debt.

\textcite{Bocola2016} confirmed to use long-term government bonds held by banks, thus the the mark-to-market channel is structurally native to his model. \textcite{Gabaix2012}'s resilience term $H_{i,t}$ is explicitly built to extend to multi-period assets, where the yield
curve is a headline result of his framework, not a side extension.
\textcite{Woodford2001} developed a geometrically-decaying-coupon perpetuity device used below. The bond return term including the payoff haircut, result in
%
\begin{equation*}
  R^b_{t+1} = \frac{(1-x_{t+1}\Delta^b)\bigl(1+\rho_b Q^b_{t+1}\bigr)}{Q^b_t}
\end{equation*}
%
where the pricing term is
\begin{equation*}
  Q^b_t = \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}(1-x_{t+1}\Delta^b)(1+\rho_b Q^b_{t+1})\bigr].
\end{equation*}
%
Solving for the steady state, the bond pricing term yields 
\begin{equation*}
  \bar Q^b = \frac{\bar H^b}{\bar R^f - \rho_b\bar H^b}
\end{equation*}
%
while the bond return at steady state results in
%
\begin{equation*}
  \bar R^b = \frac{(1-\bar\theta\Delta^b)\bar R^f}{\bar H^b}, \qquad \text{with} \qquad
  \overline{\text{spread}} = \bar R^f \cdot \frac{\bar\theta\Delta^b(\Lambda^M-1)}{\bar H^b}
\end{equation*}
%
Lastly, this alters the government budget constraint, which in real terms matches the bank's real bond pricing:
%
\begin{equation*}
  T_t + Q^b_t\bigl(B_{t+1}-\rho_b B_t\bigr) = (1-x_t\Delta^b)\,B_t
\end{equation*}

% --- Location: line 134, interbank-irrelevance paragraph ---
% CURRENT: "...operates through mark-to-market losses on sovereign
%           bond portfolios eroding individual bank net worth..."
% REPLACE WITH:
The sovereign-bank channel here operates through realised-default-triggered losses on sovereign bond portfolios eroding individual bank net worth, which is a \emph{within-bank} balance-sheet mechanism, not an \emph{across-bank} funding market mechanism.

% Could be written if we extend to long term bond: The sovereign-bank channel here operates through mark-to-market losses on sovereign bond portfolios eroding individual bank net worth, which is a \emph{within-bank} balance-sheet mechanism, not an \emph{across-bank} funding market mechanism.

% --- Location: line ~1124 (commented-out note under A.3.2.5) ---
% CURRENT (dormant): "When theta_t rises, Q^b_t falls..., eroding
%           N^b_t without any realised defaults, purely through the
%           anticipated sovereign haircut. This is the sovereign-bank
%           channel."
% ACTION: delete. Superseded by Part 1's corrected diagram and its
% accompanying paragraph, which now states the mechanism correctly
% at its proper location (A.3.3, not a margin note on A.3.2.5).

% --- Location: line 121 (commented-out hypothesis paragraph) ---
% CURRENT (dormant): "...Even without a realised haircut, an increase
%           in p_t is therefore sufficient to generate a left-tail
%           contraction..."
% REPLACE WITH, if/when this paragraph is uncommented and used as the
% paper's stated hypothesis:
The hypothesis organising this paper is that \textit{realised sovereign default risk} depresses output and tightens credit conditions through two distinct, sequentially activated channels. The \textit{asset pricing channel} operates continuously: a rise in the perceived probability $\theta_t$ of sovereign default raises the sovereign risk premium demanded by bondholders and compresses bond prices \parencite{Gabaix2012, Isore2017}, independent of whether a default ultimately occurs. The \textit{financial accelerator channel} activates only upon a realised default: losses on sovereign holdings erode bank net worth, raising the external finance premium on loans to entrepreneurs and firms, tightening credit supply, and amplifying the output contraction through the standard \textcite{Bernanke1999}-style feedback loop, here activated through bank sovereign bond holdings. A realised sovereign haircut is therefore necessary, though the elevated perceived probability that precedes it is what the sovereign spread continuously prices.

% ============================================================
% PART 3: ARCHIVED -- Long-duration bond derivation, complete
% Not currently active in the paper. Preserved here in full should
% time allow the extension. Everything below was independently
% derived and cross-checked against Gabaix (2012), Woodford (2001)-
% style perpetuity bonds, and Bocola (2016)'s own use of long-term
% government debt.
% ============================================================

% --- Literature grounding ---
% Bocola (2016) confirmed (via his own paper's stated setup) to use
% long-term government bonds held by banks -- the mark-to-market
% channel is structurally native to his model, not an approximation.
% Gabaix (2012)'s resilience H_{i,t} is explicitly built to extend to
% multi-period assets ("twisted autoregressive process"); the yield
% curve is a headline result of his framework, not a side extension.
% Woodford (2001) confirmed as the standard citation for the
% geometrically-decaying-coupon perpetuity device used below.

% --- Bond return and pricing (Option A: whole-payoff haircut,
%     chosen to match Cruces-Trebesch's present-value haircut
%     methodology already used to calibrate Delta^b) ---
\begin{equation*}
  R^b_{t+1} = \frac{(1-x_{t+1}\Delta^b)\bigl(1+\rho_b Q^b_{t+1}\bigr)}{Q^b_t}
\end{equation*}
\begin{equation*}
  Q^b_t = \mathbb{E}_t\bigl[\mathcal{M}_{t,t+1}(1-x_{t+1}\Delta^b)(1+\rho_b Q^b_{t+1})\bigr]
\end{equation*}

% --- Steady-state closed form (exact) ---
\begin{equation*}
  \bar Q^b = \frac{\bar H^b}{\bar R^f - \rho_b\bar H^b}
\end{equation*}

% --- Steady-state expected return / spread (rho_b cancels --
%     existing Delta^b calibration from the one-period bond carries
%     over unchanged; duration only activates in the dynamics) ---
\begin{equation*}
  \bar R^b = \frac{(1-\bar\theta\Delta^b)\bar R^f}{\bar H^b}, \qquad
  \overline{\text{spread}} = \bar R^f \cdot \frac{\bar\theta\Delta^b(\Lambda^M-1)}{\bar H^b}
\end{equation*}

% --- Government budget constraint, real terms (matches the bank's
%     real bond pricing rather than introducing a second nominal/real
%     bridge specific to the bond) ---
\begin{equation*}
  T_t + Q^b_t\bigl(B_{t+1}-\rho_b B_t\bigr) = (1-x_t\Delta^b)\,B_t
\end{equation*}

% --- Implementation note ---
% Recommendation: do NOT force an exact time-varying closed form for
% Q^b_t into Dynare. The dynamic recursion above drops a genuine
% covariance term between the SDF and next-period disaster
% probability (second-order, consistent with the same approximation
% already accepted for the one-period bond's closed form at third
% order). Feed Dynare the exact recursive equation directly; the
% closed form above is for steady state and calibration only.
% New parameter: rho_b in [0,1), calibrated to average euro-area
% sovereign maturity (~7-8 years / 28-32 quarters), via
% rho_b ~ Rf_bar * (1 - 1/Duration).
